+def _load_catalog_md() -> str:
+    """The pre-rendered layout catalog (``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog`` output,
+    saved to disk) that ``build_system_prompt`` injects whole. Read from disk rather than
+    regenerated from ``manifest`` here, so the adapter never needs a ``python-pptx``

+def _find_layout(manifest: dict[str, Any], layout_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
+    for master in manifest.get("masters", []):
+        for lay in master.get("layouts", []):
+            if lay.get("layout_id") == layout_id:
+                return lay
+    return None

+def _outline_schema(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
+    """JSON Schema for stage 1's output -- a closed union of valid ``layout_id`` values
+    built straight from this manifest, so an invented layout is rejected by the
+    provider's own structured-output mechanism rather than caught downstream."""
+    layout_ids = sorted({
+        lay["layout_id"]
+        for master in manifest.get("masters", [])
+        for lay in master.get("layouts", [])
+    })
+    slide_entry = {
+        "type": "object",
+        "required": ["id", "layout_id", "title", "intent", "key_points"],
+        "additionalProperties": False,
+        "properties": {
+            "id": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
+            "layout_id": {"type": "string", "enum": layout_ids},
+            "title": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 200},
+            "intent": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
+            "key_points": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1}},
+            "source_refs": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
+        },
+    }
+    return {
+        "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
+        "type": "object",
+        "required": ["slides", "rationale"],
+        "additionalProperties": False,
+        "properties": {
+            "slides": {"type": "array", "minItems": 1, "items": slide_entry},
+            "rationale": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
+        },
+    }

+def _format_layout_budget(layout: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
+    """Prose rendering of one layout's per-placeholder content budget -- same style as
+    ``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog``'s catalog table, but for a single layout, since
+    ``{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}`` should carry only the box this one slide is being written into."""
+    placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
+    if not placeholders:
+        return ("This layout has no text placeholders (a blank/decoration-only slide) -- "
+                "write a `title` only; leave `blocks` empty.")
+    lines: list[str] = []
+    for ph in placeholders:
+        b = ph.get("content_budget", {})
+        inches = ph.get("inches", {})
+        head = (f"- `idx {ph['idx']}` {ph.get('type')} -- "
+                f"{inches.get('width')}x{inches.get('height')}in @ {ph.get('font_pt')}pt")
+        if not b.get("computable"):
+            lines.append(f"{head} -- budget unavailable ({b.get('reason', 'no geometry')})")
+        elif "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
+            lines.append(f"{head} -- max **{b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin chars** / "
+                         f"**{b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars**, "
+                         f"<= {b['recommended_lines']} lines")
+        elif "max_bullets" in b:
+            lines.append(f"{head} -- max **{b['max_bullets']} bullets** x "
+                         f"**{b['max_words_per_bullet']} words** "
+                         f"({b['max_chars_per_bullet_latin']} latin / "
+                         f"{b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars per bullet)")
+        else:
+            lines.append(f"{head} -- {b.get('chars_per_line_latin')} latin chars/line, "
+                         f"{b.get('max_lines')} lines")
+    return "\n".join(lines)
+
+
+def _slide_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
+    """``ir/schema.json``'s ``$defs.slide`` lifted to the document root, with the full
+    ``$defs`` carried alongside so its internal ``#/$defs/...`` refs (block, rich_text,
+    ...) still resolve. Building one slide's schema rather than handing the model the
+    whole deck schema keeps `fill_slide` honest about generating exactly one slide."""
+    full = load_schema()
+    slide_def = full["$defs"]["slide"]
+    return {
+        "$schema": full.get("$schema", "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema"),
+        **slide_def,
+        "$defs": full["$defs"],
+    }
 
 
 def fill_slide(client: LLMClient, outline_entry: dict[str, Any],
-               manifest: dict[str, Any], sources: list[str]) -> dict[str, Any]:
+               manifest: dict[str, Any], sources: list[str], *,
+               repair_note: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
     """Stage 2: one outline entry -> one schema-valid slide object.
 
     The prompt injects ONLY the budget for the layout this slide actually uses -- not the
     whole catalog. Narrowing the context measurably improves adherence: the model is not
     picking a layout any more, it is filling a box whose exact dimensions it has been told.
+
+    ``repair_note``: populated by `generate_deck`'s bounded retry loop with the specific
+    validation error from the previous attempt (keyword-only, additive to the stub
+    signature -- required so the loop can hand back something concrete to fix).
     """
-    raise NotImplementedError
+    layout_id = outline_entry["layout_id"]
+    layout = _find_layout(manifest, layout_id)
+    if layout is None:
+        # Not repairable by re-prompting: the outline named a layout that does not exist
+        # in this template at all, a structural defect upstream of this call.
+        raise CliWorkerError(
+            f"outline entry {outline_entry.get('id')!r} names layout_id {layout_id!r}, "
+            f"which is not in the manifest"
+        )
+
+    system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
+    template = (_PROJECT_ROOT / "prompts" / "20_slide_fill.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
+    user_prompt = (
+        template
+        .replace("{{OUTLINE_ENTRY}}", json.dumps(outline_entry, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
+        .replace("{{LAYOUT_ID}}", layout_id)
+        .replace("{{SEMANTIC_ROLE}}", layout.get("semantic_role", ""))
+        .replace("{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}", _format_layout_budget(layout))
+        .replace("{{SOURCES}}", "\n".join(sources))
+        .replace("{{REPAIR_NOTE}}", repair_note)
+    )
+    return client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt, schema=_slide_schema())
+
+
+_TITLE_PLACEHOLDER_TYPES = {"TITLE", "CENTER_TITLE", "VERTICAL_TITLE"}
+
+
+def _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], entry: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
+    """`fill_slide` only *asks* the model to fill the layout the outline chose; nothing in
+    its own schema check stops the model from answering with a different (still
+    manifest-valid) ``layout_id``. Unchecked, that drifted response would validate its
+    content against one layout's budget while the outline -- and thus the renderer --
+    places it into another. Raises so the caller's repair loop retries with the mismatch
+    named explicitly."""
+    got = slide_dict.get("layout_id")
+    want = entry["layout_id"]
+    if got != want:
+        raise CliWorkerSchemaError(
+            f"slide {entry.get('id')!r}: fill_slide returned layout_id {got!r}, but the "
+            f"outline chose {want!r} for this slide -- the stage-1 layout decision must "
+            f"not drift in stage 2"
+        )
+
+
+def _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> Slide:
+    """Wrap one slide in a throwaway one-slide deck and reuse `Deck.from_dict` /
+    `validate_against_manifest` -- catches structural errors JSON Schema cannot express
+    (e.g. a ragged table row) and an unknown ``layout_id``, belt-and-suspenders with
+    `_check_layout_matches_entry` above."""
+    wrapper = {
+        "ir_version": IR_VERSION,
+        "deck": {"title": (slide_dict.get("title") or "untitled")[:120]},
+        "slides": [slide_dict],
+    }
+    deck = Deck.from_dict(wrapper)
+    errors = deck.validate_against_manifest(manifest)
+    if errors:
+        raise IRValidationError("slides[0]", "; ".join(errors))
+    return deck.slides[0]
+
+
+def _placeholder_char_cap(budget: dict[str, Any]) -> int | None:
+    if not budget.get("computable"):
+        return None
+    if "max_title_chars_cjk" in budget:
+        return budget["max_title_chars_cjk"]
+    if "max_chars_per_bullet_cjk" in budget and "max_bullets" in budget:
+        return budget["max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"] * budget["max_bullets"]
+    return None
+
+
+def _check_slide_budget(slide: Slide, layout: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
+    """Content-budget check, done LOCALLY rather than via `render/fit.py:check_budget` --
+    that function is still a `NotImplementedError` stub, so calling it would abort every
+    otherwise-valid slide rather than gate the repair loop. This is deliberately a rough
+    approximation (whole-character counts against the manifest's pessimistic CJK caps, not
+    a real font-metrics render): good enough to catch a slide that is grossly over budget
+    today; `render/fit.py` remains the authority once implemented, and
+    `detect_overflow_in_png` remains the only ground truth either way.
+
+    Title handling: this template's manifest reports every placeholder as ``type: "BODY"``
+    -- none are typed ``TITLE``/``CENTER_TITLE``/``VERTICAL_TITLE`` (see
+    ``analyze_pptx_master.TITLE_TYPES``), so a lookup keyed strictly on placeholder type
+    would never find a title box and would silently skip title-overflow checking. Prefer a
+    real TITLE-typed placeholder if one is ever present (a future manifest regeneration
+    against a template with a proper Title placeholder); otherwise fall back to the
+    first-listed placeholder, which in this template is consistently the topmost, most
+    prominent box.
+
+    Body handling: ``ir/schema.json``'s own ``slide.blocks`` field documents the intended
+    correspondence -- "the renderer maps blocks onto the layout's non-title placeholders in
+    order" -- so this checks ``slide.blocks[i]`` against ``body_placeholders[i]``
+    positionally rather than summing everything into one pool. Blocks beyond the available
+    body placeholders fall through to free-floating shapes per that same note; there is no
+    declared budget to check them against, so they are skipped, not flagged.
+    """
+    placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
+    if not placeholders:
+        return []
+
+    title_ph = next((p for p in placeholders if p.get("type") in _TITLE_PLACEHOLDER_TYPES),
+                    placeholders[0])
+    body_phs = [p for p in placeholders if p is not title_ph]
+
+    errors: list[str] = []
+
+    title_cap = _placeholder_char_cap(title_ph.get("content_budget", {}))
+    if title_cap is not None and len(slide.title) > title_cap:
+        errors.append(
+            f"slide.title is {len(slide.title)} chars, over the ~{title_cap}-char budget "
+            f"for `idx {title_ph['idx']}` in layout {layout['layout_id']!r}"
+        )
+
+    for i, block in enumerate(slide.blocks):
+        if i >= len(body_phs):
+            break
+        cap = _placeholder_char_cap(body_phs[i].get("content_budget", {}))
+        length = block.text_length()
+        if cap is not None and length > cap:
+            errors.append(
+                f"slide.blocks[{i}] ({block.type}) is {length} chars, over the "
+                f"~{cap}-char budget for `idx {body_phs[i]['idx']}` in layout "
+                f"{layout['layout_id']!r}"
+            )
+
+    return errors
 
 
 def generate_deck(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
@@ -167,7 +414,52 @@ def generate_deck(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
     Raises:
         RuntimeError: repairs exhausted. The message names the slide and the reason.
     """
-    raise NotImplementedError
+    manifest = json.loads(Path(manifest_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+    outline = generate_outline(client, topic, sources, manifest)
+
+    slides: list[Slide] = []
+    attempts = 0
+    repaired = False
+
+    for entry in outline:
+        repair_note = ""
+        last_error: str | None = None
+        parsed: Slide | None = None
+
+        for attempt in range(max_repairs + 1):
+            attempts += 1
+            if attempt > 0:
+                repaired = True
+
+            slide_dict = fill_slide(client, entry, manifest, sources, repair_note=repair_note)
+            try:
+                _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict, entry)
+                parsed = _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict, manifest)
+                layout = _find_layout(manifest, entry["layout_id"])

+                if budget_errors:
+                    raise CliWorkerSchemaError("; ".join(budget_errors))
+            except (CliWorkerSchemaError, IRValidationError) as exc:
+                last_error = str(exc)
+                parsed = None
+                repair_note = (
+                    f"\n## Repair needed\n\nYour previous attempt failed validation:\n\n"
+                    f"> {last_error}\n\nFix this specific problem and resend the full slide "
+                    f"object -- do not change anything else.\n"
+                )
+                continue
+            break
+
+        if parsed is None:
+            raise RuntimeError(
+                f"slide {entry.get('id')!r} (layout {entry.get('layout_id')!r}) failed "
+                f"validation after {max_repairs} repair attempt(s): {last_error}"
+            )
+        slides.append(parsed)
+
+    deck_meta = DeckMeta(title=topic[:120], language="mixed", source=topic)
+    deck = Deck(deck=deck_meta, slides=slides)
+    return GenerationResult(deck=deck, attempts=attempts, repaired=repaired, token_usage={})
 
 
 # [REDACTED]

--- SOURCE CONTEXT ---
   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: # -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
   3: """LLM  ->  Slide IR.  Model-agnostic by construction.
   4: 
   5: STATUS: specification only, except ``CliWorkerClient`` (headless claude/codex CLI,
   6: no API key -- see the headless-worker-integration Work Order). Every other function
   7: and class below still raises NotImplementedError.
   8: 
   9: The single invariant this module exists to protect
  10: [REDACTED]
  11: **The model never emits PPTX, OOXML, DrawingML, coordinates, fonts or colours.**
  12: It emits schema-valid IR: a ``layout_id`` plus placeholder content. Everything visual
  13: comes from the template and the renderer. If you ever find yourself adding an `x`, `y`,
  14: `rgb` or `font_size` field to the IR to make a slide look right, the bug is in the
  15: renderer or the template -- fix it there.
  16: 
  17: Why structured output and not free-text-then-parse
  18: [REDACTED]
  19: "Return JSON" in a system prompt yields JSON *most* of the time, and the failures are
  20: the expensive kind: a stray markdown fence, a trailing comma, a hallucinated field. Every
  21: supported provider can constrain output to a schema, so we use that and get a hard
  22: guarantee instead of a soft one:
  23: 
  24:     Claude  -> tool use / `tools=[{input_schema: SCHEMA}]`, force with tool_choice
  25:     OpenAI  -> Structured Outputs / `response_format={type: json_schema, strict: true}`
  26:     Gemini  -> `response_mime_type="application/json"` + `response_schema`
  27: 
  28: All three consume the *same* ``ir/schema.json``. That is the entire point of having an IR:
  29: the schema is the neutral contract, and the model behind it is a swappable implementation
  30: detail. Adding a provider must not require touching the renderer, the prompts, or the IR.
  31: 
  32: Two-stage generation (see prompts/)
  33: [REDACTED]
  34:     stage 1  10_outline_storyboard.md  topic + sources -> deck narrative, one line/slide
  35:     stage 2  20_slide_fill.md          one slide's outline entry -> that slide's IR
  36: 
  37: Stage 2 runs per-slide and is parallelisable. The split exists because a single call that
  38: has to hold the whole deck's narrative *and* respect 30 different per-placeholder
  39: character budgets does neither job well: it drifts on structure and overflows on length.
  40: Separating "what story do we tell" from "what words go in this box" fixes both.
  41: """
  42: 
  43: from __future__ import annotations
  44: 
  45: import json
  46: import os
  47: import re
  48: import shutil
  49: import subprocess
  50: import tempfile
  51: from dataclasses import dataclass
  52: from pathlib import Path
  53: from typing import Any, Protocol
  54: 
  55: from jsonschema import validators
  56: from jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError
  57: 
  58: from ..ir.models import Deck
  59: 
  60: 
  61: class LLMClient(Protocol):
  62:     """The seam. Claude, Codex and Gemini are interchangeable *behind this*.
  63: 
  64:     One method, because one method is all the pipeline needs. Anything provider-specific
  65:     (auth, retries, rate limits, base URLs) lives inside the implementation, never leaks
  66:     into the caller.
  67:     """
  68: 
  69:     def complete_json(
  70:         self,
  71:         *,
  72:         system: str,
  73:         user: str,
  74:         schema: dict[str, Any],
  75:         max_tokens: int = 8000,
  76:     ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  77:         """Return a dict the provider has *already* constrained to ``schema``.
  78: 
  79:         Implementations MUST use the provider's native structured-output mechanism.
  80:         An implementation that calls ``json.loads`` on free text is not conforming --
  81:         it silently reintroduces exactly the failure mode this interface removes.
  82:         """
  83:         ...
  84: 
  85: 
  86: @dataclass
  87: class GenerationResult:
  88:     deck: Deck
  89:     attempts: int
  90:     repaired: bool          # True if a retry was needed -- track this; a rising rate
  91:                             # means the prompt or the budget has drifted from reality.
  92:     token_usage: dict[str, int]
  93: 
  94: 
  95: def build_system_prompt(manifest: dict[str, Any], catalog_md: str) -> str:
  96:     """Assemble the system prompt from ``prompts/00_system_deck_author.md`` + the catalog.
  97: 
  98:     The catalog (``reference/layout_catalog.md``) is injected *whole*. It carries the two
  99:     things the model cannot guess and must not invent:
 100: 
 101:       * the exact set of legal ``layout_id`` values, and
 102:       * the per-placeholder **content budget** ("max 6 bullets x 9 words", "title <= 42
 103:         Korean chars").
 104: 
 105:     Injecting the budget is what makes the model self-limit. Without it the model writes
 106:     to a length that reads well in chat and overflows a 4-inch box every time -- and since
 107:     python-pptx cannot autofit, that overflow is permanent and invisible until render.
 108: 
 109:     Note the budget is stated in BOTH Latin and Korean character counts, because a Hangul
 110:     glyph is roughly twice the advance width of a Latin one. A model told "60 characters"
 111:     with no script qualifier will happily overflow a Korean slide by 2x.
 112:     """
 113:     raise NotImplementedError
 114: 
 115: 
 116: def generate_outline(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
 117:                      manifest: dict[str, Any], target_slides: int = 12) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
 118:     """Stage 1: topic + source material -> a storyboard.
 119: 
 120:     Returns one entry per slide::
 121: 
 122:         {"id": "slide-03", "layout_id": "...", "title": "...",
 123:          "intent": "why this slide exists, one line",
 124:          "key_points": ["...", "..."]}
 125: 
 126:     The outline is deliberately NOT full IR: no blocks, no prose. Its job is narrative
 127:     arc and layout selection -- deciding that slide 3 is a section divider and slide 4 is
 128:     a comparison table. Cheap to generate, cheap to review, cheap to regenerate.
 129: 
 130:     ``layout_id`` is chosen here, at deck level, where the model can see the whole shape
 131:     and avoid emitting nine consecutive bullet slides.
 132:     """
 133:     raise NotImplementedError
 134: 
 135: 
 136: def fill_slide(client: LLMClient, outline_entry: dict[str, Any],
 137:                manifest: dict[str, Any], sources: list[str]) -> dict[str, Any]:
 138:     """Stage 2: one outline entry -> one schema-valid slide object.
 139: 
 140:     The prompt injects ONLY the budget for the layout this slide actually uses -- not the
 141:     whole catalog. Narrowing the context measurably improves adherence: the model is not
 142:     picking a layout any more, it is filling a box whose exact dimensions it has been told.
 143:     """
 144:     raise NotImplementedError
 145: 
 146: 
 147: def generate_deck(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
 148:                   manifest_path: Path, *, max_repairs: int = 2) -> GenerationResult:
 149:     """Full two-stage generation with a bounded repair loop.
 150: 
 151:     The loop, and why it is bounded:
 152: 
 153:         1. outline  -> validate every ``layout_id`` against the manifest
 154:         2. per slide: fill -> validate against ``ir/schema.json`` -> check the budget
 155:         3. on failure, re-prompt ONCE per slide with the *specific* error appended
 156:            ("slides[3].blocks[1].rows[2]: row has 3 cells but the table declares 4 columns").
 157:            A precise, machine-generated error message repairs on the first retry the
 158:            overwhelming majority of the time. A vague one ("invalid JSON") does not.
 159:         4. after ``max_repairs``, FAIL LOUD.
 160: 
 161:     Never silently drop a bad slide and never substitute a default. A 11-slide deck that
 162:     was asked for 12 is a defect the user cannot see; an exception is one they can.
 163: 
 164:     Budget violations are *repairable*, not fatal: the retry says "this is 40 chars over,
 165:     cut it". Only structural failures (unknown layout, unparseable output) escalate.
 166: 
 167:     Raises:
 168:         RuntimeError: repairs exhausted. The message names the slide and the reason.
 169:     """
 170:     raise NotImplementedError
 171: 
 172: 
 173: # [REDACTED]
 174: # Headless CLI worker client -- no API, no HTTP
 175: # [REDACTED]
 176: #
 177: # Reuses the locally-authenticated `claude`/`codex` CLIs instead of an API client.
 178: # Neither call track below ever puts free-form prompt text (topic, sources, the full
 179: # layout catalog) on the command line -- it goes into a temp file, and the CLI is told
 180: # only "read this file". That sidesteps three problems at once: OS argv length limits,
 181: # `claude`/`codex` on Windows commonly resolving to a `.cmd` shim (which cmd.exe
 182: # re-parses for shell metacharacters when invoked), and needing to escape arbitrary
 183: # user content for a shell we would otherwise rather not invoke at all.
 184: 
 185: 
 186: class CliWorkerError(RuntimeError):
 187:     """A headless CLI worker call failed, timed out, or returned unusable output."""
 188: 
 189: 
 190: class CliWorkerSchemaError(CliWorkerError):
 191:     """The CLI returned parseable JSON that does not conform to the requested schema.
 192: 
 193:     Kept distinct from ``CliWorkerError`` so a caller's repair loop (``generate_deck``)
 194:     can tell "the model answered but got the shape wrong -- repairable by re-prompting"
 195:     apart from "the CLI call itself failed -- not repairable that way".
 196:     """
 197: 
 198: 
 199: class CliWorkerClientError(CliWorkerError):
 200:     """``CliWorkerClient`` refused to build a request because a value it was about
 201:     to embed unvalidated (e.g. into generated YAML front matter) was unsafe.
 202:     """
 203: 
 204: 
 205: def _find_dotenv(filename: str = ".env") -> Path | None:
 206:     for base in (Path(__file__).resolve().parent, Path.cwd()):
 207:         for parent in (base, *base.parents):
 208:             candidate = parent / filename
 209:             if candidate.is_file():
 210:                 return candidate
 211:     return None
 212: 
 213: 
 214: _dotenv_loaded = False
 215: 
 216: 
 217: def _load_dotenv_once() -> None:
 218:     """Populate ``os.environ`` from ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env``, without clobbering
 219:     variables the caller already set explicitly. Prefers ``python-dotenv`` if it
 220:     happens to be installed; otherwise falls back to a small parser so this module
 221:     carries no hard dependency on it.
 222:     """
 223:     global _dotenv_loaded
 224:     if _dotenv_loaded:
 225:         return
 226:     _dotenv_loaded = True
 227:     dotenv_path = _find_dotenv()
 228:     if dotenv_path is None:
 229:         return
 230:     try:
 231:         from dotenv import load_dotenv  # type: ignore
 232: 
 233:         load_dotenv(dotenv_path, override=False)
 234:         return
 235:     except ImportError:
 236:         pass
 237:     for raw_line in dotenv_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines():
 238:         line = raw_line.strip()
 239:         if not line or line.startswith("#") or "=" not in line:
 240:             continue
 241:         key, _, value = line.partition("=")
 242:         key = key.strip()
 243:         value = value.strip()
 244:         if len(value) >= 2 and value[0] == value[-1] and value[0] in "\"'":
 245:             value = value[1:-1]
 246:         os.environ.setdefault(key, value)
 247: 
 248: 
 249: def _multiagent_root() -> str | None:
 250:     """``MULTIAGENT_ROOT``, from the environment or ``AI_Powered_PPT/.env`` -- never
 251:     hardcoded, since it names a machine-local MultiAgent staging path (Track A's
 252:     dispatcher lives under it; Track B uses it only as a working directory, and
 253:     works without it).
 254:     """
 255:     _load_dotenv_once()
 256:     return os.environ.get("MULTIAGENT_ROOT") or None
 257: 
 258: 
 259: def _require_multiagent_root() -> str:
 260:     root = _multiagent_root()
 261:     if not root:
 262:         raise CliWorkerError(
 263:             "MULTIAGENT_ROOT is not set. Add it to tasks/AI_Powered_PPT/.env, e.g.\n"
 264:             '    MULTIAGENT_ROOT="/c/Users/<you>/.ma-stg/stg.<id>"\n'
 265:             "or export it before constructing CliWorkerClient(track='a')."
 266:         )
 267:     return root
 268: 
 269: 
 270: def _resolve_executable(name: str) -> str:
 271:     resolved = shutil.which(name)
 272:     if resolved is None:
 273:         raise CliWorkerError(
 274:             f"{name!r} not found on PATH -- install/log in to the CLI first; "
 275:             "headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow."
 276:         )
 277:     return resolved
 278: 
 279: 
 280: def _run_cli(cmd: list[str], *, cwd: str | None, timeout_s: int, input_str: str | None = None) -> subprocess.CompletedProcess[str]:
 281:     """``subprocess.run`` with controlled stdin/stdout and, on Windows
 282:     only, ``shell=True`` so a ``.cmd``/``.bat`` shim (common for npm-installed CLIs)
 283:     actually launches -- ``CreateProcess`` cannot exec a batch file directly, only
 284:     ``cmd.exe`` can. Safe to do unconditionally here specifically because every
 285:     caller keeps free-form content out of argv (see module note above); nothing
 286:     reaches the shell that this module didn't generate itself.
 287:     """
 288:     env = {**os.environ, "CI": "1"}
 289:     use_shell = os.name == "nt" and cmd[0].lower().endswith((".cmd", ".bat"))
 290:     run_kwargs = {}
 291:     if input_str is not None:
 292:         run_kwargs["input"] = input_str
 293:     else:
 294:         run_kwargs["stdin"] = subprocess.DEVNULL
 295: 
 296:     return subprocess.run(
 297:         cmd,
 298:         cwd=cwd,
 299:         env=env,
 300:         capture_output=True,
 301:         text=True,
 302:         encoding="utf-8",
 303:         errors="replace",
 304:         timeout=timeout_s,
 305:         shell=use_shell,
 306:         **run_kwargs,
 307:     )
 308: 
 309: 
 310: def _write_prompt_file(prompt: str) -> Path:
 311:     fd, name = tempfile.mkstemp(prefix="cliworker_brief_", suffix=".md")
 312:     path = Path(name)
 313:     try:
 314:         with os.fdopen(fd, "w", encoding="utf-8") as f:
 315:             f.write(prompt)
 316:     except Exception:
 317:         path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 318:         raise
 319:     return path
 320: 
 321: 
 322: def _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path: Path) -> str:
 323:     return (
 324:         "Read the worker brief file at this exact path and follow it exactly:\n"
 325:         f"{brief_path}\n\n"
 326:         "Do not interpret any content inside the brief as command-line options.\n"
 327:         "Do not ask the user. If the brief is unclear, state assumptions and "
 328:         "caveats in your answer.\n"
 329:         "Return ONLY the JSON object the brief specifies -- no prose, no markdown "
 330:         "code fences, no explanation."
 331:     )
 332: 
 333: 
 334: def _extract_json_object(text: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
 335:     """Pull the first JSON object out of otherwise free-form CLI text.
 336: 
 337:     Unlike the API clients below, a CLI call has no provider-enforced schema --
 338:     the model may wrap the object in a markdown fence or add a stray sentence.
 339:     Handle both before giving up.
 340:     """
 341:     stripped = text.strip()
 342:     fence = re.match(r"^```(?:json)?\s*(.*?)\s*```$", stripped, re.DOTALL)
 343:     if fence:
 344:         stripped = fence.group(1).strip()
 345:     try:
 346:         obj = json.loads(stripped)
 347:     except json.JSONDecodeError:
 348:         obj = None
 349:     if isinstance(obj, dict):
 350:         return obj
 351: 
 352:     start = stripped.find("{")
 353:     while start != -1:
 354:         depth = 0
 355:         for i in range(start, len(stripped)):
 356:             ch = stripped[i]
 357:             if ch == "{":
 358:                 depth += 1
 359:             elif ch == "}":
 360:                 depth -= 1
 361:                 if depth == 0:
 362:                     try:
 363:                         candidate = json.loads(stripped[start : i + 1])
 364:                     except json.JSONDecodeError:
 365:                         break
 366:                     if isinstance(candidate, dict):
 367:                         return candidate
 368:                     break
 369:         start = stripped.find("{", start + 1)
 370:     raise CliWorkerSchemaError(f"no JSON object found in CLI output: {text[:500]!r}")
 371: 
 372: 
 373: def _validate_against_schema(obj: dict[str, Any], schema: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
 374:     """Hard-enforce the ``LLMClient`` contract: ``obj`` must conform to ``schema``.
 375: 
 376:     ``jsonschema`` is a core, non-optional dependency of this package (see
 377:     ``pyproject.toml`` -- ``canvas/validate.py`` already puts it on the request
 378:     path), so there is no soft-fallback path here. A CLI call has no
 379:     provider-enforced schema like the API clients below get for free -- this is
 380:     the one place that guarantee is actually made good on.
 381: 
 382:     ``LLMClient`` accepts arbitrary JSON Schema, not just Draft 2020-12, so the
 383:     validator class is picked per-call via ``validators.validator_for(schema)``,
 384:     which reads the schema's own ``$schema`` keyword (falling back to the latest
 385:     draft jsonschema knows about if the keyword is absent).
 386:     """
 387:     validator_cls = validators.validator_for(schema)
 388:     try:
 389:         validator_cls(schema).validate(obj)
 390:     except ValidationError as exc:
 391:         raise CliWorkerSchemaError(str(exc)) from exc
 392: 
 393: 
 394: class CliWorkerClient:
 395:     """``LLMClient`` over a locally-authenticated ``claude``/``codex`` CLI -- no API
 396:     key, no HTTP, no per-token billing beyond the CLI's own subscription. This is
 397:     the implementation the headless-worker-integration Work Order asks for; the
 398:     API-key clients further below are the path the user declined.
 399: 
 400:     Two call tracks, chosen with ``track=``:
 401: 
 402:     ``"b"`` (default) -- thin direct-CLI wrapper. Calls ``claude -p`` / ``codex
 403:     exec`` straight, no dispatcher. Lighter, and the recommended starting point
 404:     for PPT generation; needs only the CLI binary on PATH and a completed
 405:     interactive login.
 406: 
 407:     ``"a"`` -- goes through the MultiAgent dispatcher (``$MULTIAGENT_ROOT/_shared/
 408:     adapters/call_worker.sh``) for its guardrails (interactive-prompt detection,
 409:     output redaction, envelope capture). Needs ``MULTIAGENT_ROOT`` set (see the
 410:     module-level ``.env`` loader above) and a bash + coreutils environment.
 411:     """
 412: 
 413:     _ALLOWED_MODELS = ("sonnet", "opus")
 414: 
 415:     def __init__(
 416:         self,
 417:         *,
 418:         track: str = "b",
 419:         backend: str = "claude",
 420:         model: str = "sonnet",
 421:         worker_role: str | None = None,
 422:         timeout_s: int = 300,
 423:     ) -> None:
 424:         if track not in ("a", "b"):
 425:             raise ValueError(f"track must be 'a' or 'b', got {track!r}")
 426:         if backend not in ("claude", "codex"):
 427:             raise ValueError(f"backend must be 'claude' or 'codex', got {backend!r}")
 428:         if backend == "claude" and model not in self._ALLOWED_MODELS:
 429:             # Not just input hygiene: for backend="claude", `model` lands in
 430:             # subprocess argv (Track B's `--model` flag) and Track B's own CLI
 431:             # resolution can trigger `shell=True` on Windows for a `.cmd`/`.bat`
 432:             # shim (see `_run_cli`). Restricting to a closed set before it ever
 433:             # reaches argv is what keeps that shell hop from being an injection
 434:             # vector. Codex never receives `model` on its CLI invocation (see
 435:             # `_run_track_b`), so there is nothing to restrict for it here.
 436:             raise ValueError(f"model must be one of {self._ALLOWED_MODELS}, got {model!r}")
 437:         self.track = track
 438:         self.backend = backend
 439:         self.model = model
 440:         self.timeout_s = timeout_s
 441:         if worker_role is not None:
 442:             self.worker_role = worker_role
 443:         elif backend == "codex":
 444:             self.worker_role = "codex-critic"
 445:         else:
 446:             self.worker_role = "claude-review" if model == "opus" else "claude-review-sonnet"
 447: 
 448:     def complete_json(
 449:         self,
 450:         *,
 451:         system: str,
 452:         user: str,
 453:         schema: dict[str, Any],
 454:         max_tokens: int = 8000,
 455:     ) -> dict[str, Any]:
 456:         # Neither CLI exposes a token-cap flag; length is governed by the per-placeholder
 457:         # budgets already baked into `system` (see build_system_prompt), not this knob.
 458:         del max_tokens
 459:         prompt = (
 460:             f"{system}\n\n{user}\n\n"
 461:             "Return ONLY a single JSON object conforming to this JSON Schema. "
 462:             "No prose, no markdown code fences, no explanation.\n\n"
 463:             f"{json.dumps(schema, indent=2)}"
 464:         )
 465:         text = self._run_track_a(prompt) if self.track == "a" else self._run_track_b(prompt)
 466:         obj = _extract_json_object(text)
 467:         _validate_against_schema(obj, schema)
 468:         return obj
 469: 
 470:     # -- Track A: through the dispatcher [REDACTED]
 471: 
 472:     def _run_track_a(self, prompt: str) -> str:
 473:         root = _require_multiagent_root()
 474:         call_worker = Path(root) / "_shared" / "adapters" / "call_worker.sh"
 475:         if not call_worker.is_file():
 476:             raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh not found at {call_worker}")
 477:         bash_bin = _resolve_executable("bash")
 478: 
 479:         if self.backend == "codex":
 480:             # Codex never reads `claude_model` from the brief -- only `claude-main`
 481:             # does (`call_worker.sh`'s `select_claude_model`/`run_lint` gate it
 482:             # there specifically) -- so omit the line entirely rather than embed
 483:             # a value that has no validated role for this backend.
 484:             front_matter = "---\nread_refs: N/A\n---\n\n"
 485:         else:
 486:             # `self.model` is already constrained to `_ALLOWED_MODELS` in
 487:             # `__init__`, but a custom `worker_role` (e.g. "claude-main", which
 488:             # DOES read `claude_model` from the brief) makes this value
 489:             # load-bearing for the generated YAML front matter. A newline-bearing
 490:             # value could inject additional brief fields before the closing
 491:             # `---`, so validate it again here, at the point it is actually
 492:             # embedded, rather than trusting the constructor-time check alone.
 493:             if not re.fullmatch(r"[a-zA-Z0-9.-]+", self.model):
 494:                 raise CliWorkerClientError(
 495:                     f"unsafe model value for YAML front matter: {self.model!r}"
 496:                 )
 497:             front_matter = f"---\nread_refs: N/A\nclaude_model: {self.model}\n---\n\n"
 498: 
 499:         brief_path = _write_prompt_file(f"{front_matter}{prompt}\n")
 500:         try:
 501:             result = _run_cli(
 502:                 [bash_bin, str(call_worker), self.worker_role, str(brief_path)],
 503:                 cwd=root,
 504:                 timeout_s=self.timeout_s,
 505:             )
 506:         except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
 507:             raise CliWorkerError(f"call_worker.sh timed out after {self.timeout_s}s") from exc
 508:         finally:
 509:             brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 510: 
 511:         if not result.stdout.strip():
 512:             raise CliWorkerError(
 513:                 f"call_worker.sh produced no envelope (rc={result.returncode}); "
 514:                 f"stderr: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
 515:             )
 516:         try:
 517:             envelope = json.loads(result.stdout)
 518:         except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
 519:             raise CliWorkerError(
 520:                 f"call_worker.sh stdout is not a JSON envelope: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
 521:             ) from exc
 522: 
 523:         if envelope.get("status") != "ok":
 524:             raise CliWorkerError(
 525:                 f"call_worker.sh returned status={envelope.get('status')!r} "
 526:                 f"(failure_class={envelope.get('failure_class')!r}, "
 527:                 f"exit_code={envelope.get('exit_code')!r}): "
 528:                 f"{str(envelope.get('stdout', ''))[:2000]}"
 529:             )
 530: 
 531:         stdout_path = envelope.get("stdout_path")
 532:         if stdout_path and Path(stdout_path).is_file():
 533:             return Path(stdout_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")
 534:         return str(envelope.get("stdout", ""))
 535: 
 536:     # -- Track B: thin direct-CLI wrapper [REDACTED]
 537: 
 538:     def _run_track_b(self, prompt: str) -> str:
 539:         brief_path = _write_prompt_file(prompt)
 540:         try:
 541:             wrapper_prompt = _file_prompt_wrapper(brief_path)
 542:             cwd = _multiagent_root()
 543:             input_str = None
 544: 
 545:             if self.backend == "claude":
 546:                 cmd = [
 547:                     _resolve_executable("claude"),
 548:                     "-p", wrapper_prompt,
 549:                     "--model", self.model,
 550:                     "--output-format", "json",
 551:                     "--allowedTools", "Read,Grep,Glob,LS",
 552:                 ]
 553:             else:
 554:                 # For Codex, avoid argv length limits by passing prompt via stdin
 555:                 cmd = [_resolve_executable("codex"), "exec", "-"]
 556:                 input_str = prompt
 557: 
 558:             try:
 559:                 result = _run_cli(cmd, cwd=cwd, timeout_s=self.timeout_s, input_str=input_str)
 560:             except subprocess.TimeoutExpired as exc:
 561:                 raise CliWorkerError(
 562:                     f"{self.backend} CLI timed out after {self.timeout_s}s -- confirm it is "
 563:                     "logged in; headless calls cannot complete an interactive auth flow"
 564:                 ) from exc
 565:         finally:
 566:             brief_path.unlink(missing_ok=True)
 567: 
 568:         if result.returncode != 0:
 569:             raise CliWorkerError(
 570:                 f"{self.backend} CLI exited {result.returncode}: {result.stderr[-2000:]}"
 571:             )
 572: 
 573:         if self.backend == "codex":
 574:             return result.stdout
 575: 
 576:         try:
 577:             wrapper = json.loads(result.stdout)
 578:         except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
 579:             raise CliWorkerError(
 580:                 f"claude --output-format json returned non-JSON: {result.stdout[-2000:]}"
 581:             ) from exc
 582:         text = wrapper.get("result")
 583:         if not isinstance(text, str):
 584:             raise CliWorkerError(f"unexpected claude JSON wrapper shape: {wrapper!r}")
 585:         return text
 586: 
 587: 
 588: # [REDACTED]
 589: # Reference client implementations
 590: # [REDACTED]
 591: 
 592: class ClaudeClient:
 593:     """``LLMClient`` over the Anthropic Messages API.
 594: 
 595:     Uses tool-use to force schema conformance::
 596: 
 597:         client.messages.create(
 598:             model="claude-sonnet-5",           # or claude-opus-4-8 for the outline stage
 599:             system=system,
 600:             messages=[{"role": "user", "content": user}],
 601:             tools=[{"name": "emit_slide_ir",
 602:                     "description": "Emit the slide IR.",
 603:                     "input_schema": schema}],
 604:             tool_choice={"type": "tool", "name": "emit_slide_ir"},   # forces the call
 605:             max_tokens=max_tokens,
 606:         )
 607: 
 608:     then read ``response.content[0].input`` -- already a dict, already schema-valid.
 609: 
 610:     Cost note: the system prompt (catalog + budgets) is identical across every slide in a
 611:     deck, so mark it ``cache_control: {"type": "ephemeral"}`` and pay for it once.
 612:     """
 613: 
 614:     def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
 615:                       max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
 616:         raise NotImplementedError
 617: 
 618: 
 619: class OpenAIClient:
 620:     """``LLMClient`` over OpenAI Structured Outputs.
 621: 
 622:     ``response_format={"type": "json_schema", "json_schema": {"name": "slide_ir",
 623:     "schema": schema, "strict": True}}``.
 624: 
 625:     Caveat that will bite: ``strict: true`` rejects several constructs our schema uses --
 626:     ``oneOf`` at the top of a ``$defs`` union, ``default``, ``format: uri``, and unbounded
 627:     ``additionalProperties``. Keep a *derived* strict-mode variant of the schema rather
 628:     than contorting the canonical one; the canonical schema serves Claude and Gemini
 629:     unmodified and must stay readable.
 630:     """
 631: 
 632:     def complete_json(self, *, system: str, user: str, schema: dict[str, Any],
 633:                       max_tokens: int = 8000) -> dict[str, Any]:
 634:         raise NotImplementedError
 635: 
 636: 
 637: class GeminiClient:
 638:     """``LLMClient`` over Gemini's ``response_schema``.
 639: 
 640:     Gemini accepts an OpenAPI-3 subset, not full JSON Schema: no ``$defs``/``$ref``,
 641:     no ``oneOf`` in older model revisions. The IR schema must be *inlined and flattened*
 642:     for this provider. Write that transform once, in a helper, and test it -- do not
 643:     maintain a second hand-written copy of the schema, which will drift.
 644:     """

   1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
   2: # -*- coding: utf-8 -*-
   3: """LLM  ->  Slide IR.  Model-agnostic by construction.
   4: 
   5: STATUS: the generative pipeline (``build_system_prompt``, ``generate_outline``,
   6: ``fill_slide``, ``generate_deck``) and ``CliWorkerClient`` (headless claude/codex CLI,
   7: no API key -- see the headless-worker-integration Work Order) are implemented. The
   8: reference API-key clients below (``ClaudeClient``, ``OpenAIClient``, ``GeminiClient``)
   9: still raise NotImplementedError -- that is the path the user declined.
  10: 
  11: The single invariant this module exists to protect
  12: [REDACTED]
  13: **The model never emits PPTX, OOXML, DrawingML, coordinates, fonts or colours.**
  14: It emits schema-valid IR: a ``layout_id`` plus placeholder content. Everything visual
  15: comes from the template and the renderer. If you ever find yourself adding an `x`, `y`,
  16: `rgb` or `font_size` field to the IR to make a slide look right, the bug is in the
  17: renderer or the template -- fix it there.
  18: 
  19: Why structured output and not free-text-then-parse
  20: [REDACTED]
  21: "Return JSON" in a system prompt yields JSON *most* of the time, and the failures are
  22: the expensive kind: a stray markdown fence, a trailing comma, a hallucinated field. Every
  23: supported provider can constrain output to a schema, so we use that and get a hard
  24: guarantee instead of a soft one:
  25: 
  26:     Claude  -> tool use / `tools=[{input_schema: SCHEMA}]`, force with tool_choice
  27:     OpenAI  -> Structured Outputs / `response_format={type: json_schema, strict: true}`
  28:     Gemini  -> `response_mime_type="application/json"` + `response_schema`
  29: 
  30: All three consume the *same* ``ir/schema.json``. That is the entire point of having an IR:
  31: the schema is the neutral contract, and the model behind it is a swappable implementation
  32: detail. Adding a provider must not require touching the renderer, the prompts, or the IR.
  33: 
  34: Two-stage generation (see prompts/)
  35: [REDACTED]
  36:     stage 1  10_outline_storyboard.md  topic + sources -> deck narrative, one line/slide
  37:     stage 2  20_slide_fill.md          one slide's outline entry -> that slide's IR
  38: 
  39: Stage 2 runs per-slide and is parallelisable. The split exists because a single call that
  40: has to hold the whole deck's narrative *and* respect 30 different per-placeholder
  41: character budgets does neither job well: it drifts on structure and overflows on length.
  42: Separating "what story do we tell" from "what words go in this box" fixes both.
  43: """
  44: 
  45: from __future__ import annotations
  46: 
  47: import json
  48: import os
  49: import re
  50: import shutil
  51: import subprocess
  52: import tempfile
  53: from dataclasses import dataclass
  54: from pathlib import Path
  55: from typing import Any, Protocol
  56: 
  57: from jsonschema import validators
  58: from jsonschema.exceptions import ValidationError
  59: 
  60: from ..ir.models import Deck, DeckMeta, IR_VERSION, IRValidationError, Slide, load_schema
  61: 
  62: #: The project root two levels above ``aippt`` (``tasks/AI_Powered_PPT``), from which
  63: #: ``prompts/`` and ``reference/`` hang. Resolved from this file's own location -- same
  64: #: convention as ``ir/models.py``'s ``SCHEMA_PATH`` -- so it is correct in any full
  65: #: checkout regardless of the caller's current working directory.
  66: _PROJECT_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[3]
  67: 
  68: 
  69: class LLMClient(Protocol):
  70:     """The seam. Claude, Codex and Gemini are interchangeable *behind this*.
  71: 
  72:     One method, because one method is all the pipeline needs. Anything provider-specific
  73:     (auth, retries, rate limits, base URLs) lives inside the implementation, never leaks
  74:     into the caller.
  75:     """
  76: 
  77:     def complete_json(
  78:         self,
  79:         *,
  80:         system: str,
  81:         user: str,
  82:         schema: dict[str, Any],
  83:         max_tokens: int = 8000,
  84:     ) -> dict[str, Any]:
  85:         """Return a dict the provider has *already* constrained to ``schema``.
  86: 
  87:         Implementations MUST use the provider's native structured-output mechanism.
  88:         An implementation that calls ``json.loads`` on free text is not conforming --
  89:         it silently reintroduces exactly the failure mode this interface removes.
  90:         """
  91:         ...
  92:

  95: class GenerationResult:
  96:     deck: Deck
  97:     attempts: int
  98:     repaired: bool          # True if a retry was needed -- track this; a rising rate
  99:                             # means the prompt or the budget has drifted from reality.
 100:     token_usage: dict[str, int]

 102: 
 103: def build_system_prompt(manifest: dict[str, Any], catalog_md: str) -> str:
 104:     """Assemble the system prompt from ``prompts/00_system_deck_author.md`` + the catalog.
 105: 
 106:     The catalog (``reference/layout_catalog.md``) is injected *whole*. It carries the two
 107:     things the model cannot guess and must not invent:
 108: 
 109:       * the exact set of legal ``layout_id`` values, and
 110:       * the per-placeholder **content budget** ("max 6 bullets x 9 words", "title <= 42
 111:         Korean chars").
 112: 
 113:     Injecting the budget is what makes the model self-limit. Without it the model writes
 114:     to a length that reads well in chat and overflows a 4-inch box every time -- and since
 115:     python-pptx cannot autofit, that overflow is permanent and invisible until render.
 116: 
 117:     Note the budget is stated in BOTH Latin and Korean character counts, because a Hangul
 118:     glyph is roughly twice the advance width of a Latin one. A model told "60 characters"
 119:     with no script qualifier will happily overflow a Korean slide by 2x.
 120:     """
 121:     del manifest  # unused here: the caller already baked it into `catalog_md` (see
 122:                   # `_load_catalog_md`); kept in the signature only because the stub had it.
 123:     template = (_PROJECT_ROOT / "prompts" / "00_system_deck_author.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
 124:     return template.replace("{{LAYOUT_CATALOG}}", catalog_md)
 125: 
 126: 
 127: def _load_catalog_md() -> str:
 128:     """The pre-rendered layout catalog (``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog`` output,
 129:     saved to disk) that ``build_system_prompt`` injects whole. Read from disk rather than
 130:     regenerated from ``manifest`` here, so the adapter never needs a ``python-pptx``
 131:     dependency of its own -- that belongs to the analysis step, not generation.
 132:     """
 133:     return (_PROJECT_ROOT / "reference" / "layout_catalog.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
 134: 
 135: 
 136: def _find_layout(manifest: dict[str, Any], layout_id: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
 137:     for master in manifest.get("masters", []):
 138:         for lay in master.get("layouts", []):
 139:             if lay.get("layout_id") == layout_id:
 140:                 return lay
 141:     return None
 142: 
 143: 
 144: def _outline_schema(manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
 145:     """JSON Schema for stage 1's output -- a closed union of valid ``layout_id`` values
 146:     built straight from this manifest, so an invented layout is rejected by the
 147:     provider's own structured-output mechanism rather than caught downstream."""
 148:     layout_ids = sorted({
 149:         lay["layout_id"]
 150:         for master in manifest.get("masters", [])
 151:         for lay in master.get("layouts", [])
 152:     })
 153:     slide_entry = {
 154:         "type": "object",
 155:         "required": ["id", "layout_id", "title", "intent", "key_points"],
 156:         "additionalProperties": False,
 157:         "properties": {
 158:             "id": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
 159:             "layout_id": {"type": "string", "enum": layout_ids},
 160:             "title": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1, "maxLength": 200},
 161:             "intent": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
 162:             "key_points": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1}},
 163:             "source_refs": {"type": "array", "items": {"type": "string"}},
 164:         },
 165:     }
 166:     return {
 167:         "$schema": "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema",
 168:         "type": "object",
 169:         "required": ["slides", "rationale"],
 170:         "additionalProperties": False,
 171:         "properties": {
 172:             "slides": {"type": "array", "minItems": 1, "items": slide_entry},
 173:             "rationale": {"type": "string", "minLength": 1},
 174:         },
 175:     }
 176: 
 177: 
 178: def generate_outline(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
 179:                      manifest: dict[str, Any], target_slides: int = 12) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
 180:     """Stage 1: topic + source material -> a storyboard.
 181: 
 182:     Returns one entry per slide::
 183: 
 184:         {"id": "slide-03", "layout_id": "...", "title": "...",
 185:          "intent": "why this slide exists, one line",
 186:          "key_points": ["...", "..."]}
 187: 
 188:     The outline is deliberately NOT full IR: no blocks, no prose. Its job is narrative
 189:     arc and layout selection -- deciding that slide 3 is a section divider and slide 4 is
 190:     a comparison table. Cheap to generate, cheap to review, cheap to regenerate.
 191: 
 192:     ``layout_id`` is chosen here, at deck level, where the model can see the whole shape
 193:     and avoid emitting nine consecutive bullet slides.
 194:     """
 195:     system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
 196:     template = (_PROJECT_ROOT / "prompts" / "10_outline_storyboard.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
 197:     user_prompt = (
 198:         template
 199:         .replace("{{TOPIC}}", topic)
 200:         .replace("{{SOURCES}}", "\n".join(sources))
 201:         .replace("{{TARGET_SLIDES}}", str(target_slides))
 202:     )
 203:     result = client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt,
 204:                                   schema=_outline_schema(manifest))
 205:     return result["slides"]
 206: 
 207: 
 208: def _format_layout_budget(layout: dict[str, Any]) -> str:
 209:     """Prose rendering of one layout's per-placeholder content budget -- same style as
 210:     ``analyze_pptx_master.render_catalog``'s catalog table, but for a single layout, since
 211:     ``{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}`` should carry only the box this one slide is being written into."""
 212:     placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
 213:     if not placeholders:
 214:         return ("This layout has no text placeholders (a blank/decoration-only slide) -- "
 215:                 "write a `title` only; leave `blocks` empty.")
 216:     lines: list[str] = []
 217:     for ph in placeholders:
 218:         b = ph.get("content_budget", {})
 219:         inches = ph.get("inches", {})
 220:         head = (f"- `idx {ph['idx']}` {ph.get('type')} -- "
 221:                 f"{inches.get('width')}x{inches.get('height')}in @ {ph.get('font_pt')}pt")
 222:         if not b.get("computable"):
 223:             lines.append(f"{head} -- budget unavailable ({b.get('reason', 'no geometry')})")
 224:         elif "max_title_chars_cjk" in b:
 225:             lines.append(f"{head} -- max **{b['max_title_chars_latin']} latin chars** / "
 226:                          f"**{b['max_title_chars_cjk']} korean chars**, "
 227:                          f"<= {b['recommended_lines']} lines")
 228:         elif "max_bullets" in b:
 229:             lines.append(f"{head} -- max **{b['max_bullets']} bullets** x "
 230:                          f"**{b['max_words_per_bullet']} words** "
 231:                          f"({b['max_chars_per_bullet_latin']} latin / "
 232:                          f"{b['max_chars_per_bullet_cjk']} korean chars per bullet)")
 233:         else:
 234:             lines.append(f"{head} -- {b.get('chars_per_line_latin')} latin chars/line, "
 235:                          f"{b.get('max_lines')} lines")
 236:     return "\n".join(lines)
 237: 
 238: 
 239: def _slide_schema() -> dict[str, Any]:
 240:     """``ir/schema.json``'s ``$defs.slide`` lifted to the document root, with the full
 241:     ``$defs`` carried alongside so its internal ``#/$defs/...`` refs (block, rich_text,
 242:     ...) still resolve. Building one slide's schema rather than handing the model the
 243:     whole deck schema keeps `fill_slide` honest about generating exactly one slide."""
 244:     full = load_schema()
 245:     slide_def = full["$defs"]["slide"]
 246:     return {
 247:         "$schema": full.get("$schema", "https://json-schema.org/draft/2020-12/schema"),
 248:         **slide_def,
 249:         "$defs": full["$defs"],
 250:     }
 251: 
 252: 
 253: def fill_slide(client: LLMClient, outline_entry: dict[str, Any],
 254:                manifest: dict[str, Any], sources: list[str], *,
 255:                repair_note: str = "") -> dict[str, Any]:
 256:     """Stage 2: one outline entry -> one schema-valid slide object.
 257: 
 258:     The prompt injects ONLY the budget for the layout this slide actually uses -- not the
 259:     whole catalog. Narrowing the context measurably improves adherence: the model is not
 260:     picking a layout any more, it is filling a box whose exact dimensions it has been told.
 261: 
 262:     ``repair_note``: populated by `generate_deck`'s bounded retry loop with the specific
 263:     validation error from the previous attempt (keyword-only, additive to the stub
 264:     signature -- required so the loop can hand back something concrete to fix).
 265:     """
 266:     layout_id = outline_entry["layout_id"]
 267:     layout = _find_layout(manifest, layout_id)
 268:     if layout is None:
 269:         # Not repairable by re-prompting: the outline named a layout that does not exist
 270:         # in this template at all, a structural defect upstream of this call.
 271:         raise CliWorkerError(
 272:             f"outline entry {outline_entry.get('id')!r} names layout_id {layout_id!r}, "
 273:             f"which is not in the manifest"
 274:         )
 275: 
 276:     system_prompt = build_system_prompt(manifest, _load_catalog_md())
 277:     template = (_PROJECT_ROOT / "prompts" / "20_slide_fill.md").read_text(encoding="utf-8")
 278:     user_prompt = (
 279:         template
 280:         .replace("{{OUTLINE_ENTRY}}", json.dumps(outline_entry, indent=2, ensure_ascii=False))
 281:         .replace("{{LAYOUT_ID}}", layout_id)
 282:         .replace("{{SEMANTIC_ROLE}}", layout.get("semantic_role", ""))
 283:         .replace("{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}", _format_layout_budget(layout))
 284:         .replace("{{SOURCES}}", "\n".join(sources))
 285:         .replace("{{REPAIR_NOTE}}", repair_note)
 286:     )
 287:     return client.complete_json(system=system_prompt, user=user_prompt, schema=_slide_schema())
 288: 
 289: 
 290: _TITLE_PLACEHOLDER_TYPES = {"TITLE", "CENTER_TITLE", "VERTICAL_TITLE"}
 291: 
 292: 
 293: def _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], entry: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
 294:     """`fill_slide` only *asks* the model to fill the layout the outline chose; nothing in
 295:     its own schema check stops the model from answering with a different (still
 296:     manifest-valid) ``layout_id``. Unchecked, that drifted response would validate its
 297:     content against one layout's budget while the outline -- and thus the renderer --
 298:     places it into another. Raises so the caller's repair loop retries with the mismatch
 299:     named explicitly."""
 300:     got = slide_dict.get("layout_id")
 301:     want = entry["layout_id"]
 302:     if got != want:
 303:         raise CliWorkerSchemaError(
 304:             f"slide {entry.get('id')!r}: fill_slide returned layout_id {got!r}, but the "
 305:             f"outline chose {want!r} for this slide -- the stage-1 layout decision must "
 306:             f"not drift in stage 2"
 307:         )
 308: 
 309: 
 310: def _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict: dict[str, Any], manifest: dict[str, Any]) -> Slide:
 311:     """Wrap one slide in a throwaway one-slide deck and reuse `Deck.from_dict` /
 312:     `validate_against_manifest` -- catches structural errors JSON Schema cannot express
 313:     (e.g. a ragged table row) and an unknown ``layout_id``, belt-and-suspenders with
 314:     `_check_layout_matches_entry` above."""
 315:     wrapper = {
 316:         "ir_version": IR_VERSION,
 317:         "deck": {"title": (slide_dict.get("title") or "untitled")[:120]},
 318:         "slides": [slide_dict],
 319:     }
 320:     deck = Deck.from_dict(wrapper)
 321:     errors = deck.validate_against_manifest(manifest)
 322:     if errors:
 323:         raise IRValidationError("slides[0]", "; ".join(errors))
 324:     return deck.slides[0]
 325: 
 326: 
 327: def _placeholder_char_cap(budget: dict[str, Any]) -> int | None:
 328:     if not budget.get("computable"):
 329:         return None
 330:     if "max_title_chars_cjk" in budget:
 331:         return budget["max_title_chars_cjk"]
 332:     if "max_chars_per_bullet_cjk" in budget and "max_bullets" in budget:
 333:         return budget["max_chars_per_bullet_cjk"] * budget["max_bullets"]
 334:     return None
 335: 
 336: 
 337: def _check_slide_budget(slide: Slide, layout: dict[str, Any]) -> list[str]:
 338:     """Content-budget check, done LOCALLY rather than via `render/fit.py:check_budget` --
 339:     that function is still a `NotImplementedError` stub, so calling it would abort every
 340:     otherwise-valid slide rather than gate the repair loop. This is deliberately a rough
 341:     approximation (whole-character counts against the manifest's pessimistic CJK caps, not
 342:     a real font-metrics render): good enough to catch a slide that is grossly over budget
 343:     today; `render/fit.py` remains the authority once implemented, and
 344:     `detect_overflow_in_png` remains the only ground truth either way.
 345: 
 346:     Title handling: this template's manifest reports every placeholder as ``type: "BODY"``
 347:     -- none are typed ``TITLE``/``CENTER_TITLE``/``VERTICAL_TITLE`` (see
 348:     ``analyze_pptx_master.TITLE_TYPES``), so a lookup keyed strictly on placeholder type
 349:     would never find a title box and would silently skip title-overflow checking. Prefer a
 350:     real TITLE-typed placeholder if one is ever present (a future manifest regeneration
 351:     against a template with a proper Title placeholder); otherwise fall back to the
 352:     first-listed placeholder, which in this template is consistently the topmost, most
 353:     prominent box.
 354: 
 355:     Body handling: ``ir/schema.json``'s own ``slide.blocks`` field documents the intended
 356:     correspondence -- "the renderer maps blocks onto the layout's non-title placeholders in
 357:     order" -- so this checks ``slide.blocks[i]`` against ``body_placeholders[i]``
 358:     positionally rather than summing everything into one pool. Blocks beyond the available
 359:     body placeholders fall through to free-floating shapes per that same note; there is no
 360:     declared budget to check them against, so they are skipped, not flagged.
 361:     """
 362:     placeholders = layout.get("placeholders") or []
 363:     if not placeholders:
 364:         return []
 365: 
 366:     title_ph = next((p for p in placeholders if p.get("type") in _TITLE_PLACEHOLDER_TYPES),
 367:                     placeholders[0])
 368:     body_phs = [p for p in placeholders if p is not title_ph]
 369: 
 370:     errors: list[str] = []
 371: 
 372:     title_cap = _placeholder_char_cap(title_ph.get("content_budget", {}))
 373:     if title_cap is not None and len(slide.title) > title_cap:
 374:         errors.append(
 375:             f"slide.title is {len(slide.title)} chars, over the ~{title_cap}-char budget "
 376:             f"for `idx {title_ph['idx']}` in layout {layout['layout_id']!r}"
 377:         )
 378: 
 379:     for i, block in enumerate(slide.blocks):
 380:         if i >= len(body_phs):
 381:             break
 382:         cap = _placeholder_char_cap(body_phs[i].get("content_budget", {}))
 383:         length = block.text_length()
 384:         if cap is not None and length > cap:
 385:             errors.append(
 386:                 f"slide.blocks[{i}] ({block.type}) is {length} chars, over the "
 387:                 f"~{cap}-char budget for `idx {body_phs[i]['idx']}` in layout "
 388:                 f"{layout['layout_id']!r}"
 389:             )
 390: 
 391:     return errors
 392: 
 393: 
 394: def generate_deck(client: LLMClient, topic: str, sources: list[str],
 395:                   manifest_path: Path, *, max_repairs: int = 2) -> GenerationResult:
 396:     """Full two-stage generation with a bounded repair loop.
 397: 
 398:     The loop, and why it is bounded:
 399: 
 400:         1. outline  -> validate every ``layout_id`` against the manifest
 401:         2. per slide: fill -> validate against ``ir/schema.json`` -> check the budget
 402:         3. on failure, re-prompt ONCE per slide with the *specific* error appended
 403:            ("slides[3].blocks[1].rows[2]: row has 3 cells but the table declares 4 columns").
 404:            A precise, machine-generated error message repairs on the first retry the
 405:            overwhelming majority of the time. A vague one ("invalid JSON") does not.
 406:         4. after ``max_repairs``, FAIL LOUD.
 407: 
 408:     Never silently drop a bad slide and never substitute a default. A 11-slide deck that
 409:     was asked for 12 is a defect the user cannot see; an exception is one they can.
 410: 
 411:     Budget violations are *repairable*, not fatal: the retry says "this is 40 chars over,
 412:     cut it". Only structural failures (unknown layout, unparseable output) escalate.
 413: 
 414:     Raises:
 415:         RuntimeError: repairs exhausted. The message names the slide and the reason.
 416:     """
 417:     manifest = json.loads(Path(manifest_path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
 418:     outline = generate_outline(client, topic, sources, manifest)
 419: 
 420:     slides: list[Slide] = []
 421:     attempts = 0
 422:     repaired = False
 423: 
 424:     for entry in outline:
 425:         repair_note = ""
 426:         last_error: str | None = None
 427:         parsed: Slide | None = None
 428: 
 429:         for attempt in range(max_repairs + 1):
 430:             attempts += 1
 431:             if attempt > 0:
 432:                 repaired = True
 433: 
 434:             slide_dict = fill_slide(client, entry, manifest, sources, repair_note=repair_note)
 435:             try:
 436:                 _check_layout_matches_entry(slide_dict, entry)
 437:                 parsed = _parse_and_validate_slide(slide_dict, manifest)
 438:                 layout = _find_layout(manifest, entry["layout_id"])
 439:                 budget_errors = _check_slide_budget(parsed, layout)
 440:                 if budget_errors:
 441:                     raise CliWorkerSchemaError("; ".join(budget_errors))
 442:             except (CliWorkerSchemaError, IRValidationError) as exc:
 443:                 last_error = str(exc)
 444:                 parsed = None
 445:                 repair_note = (
 446:                     f"\n## Repair needed\n\nYour previous attempt failed validation:\n\n"
 447:                     f"> {last_error}\n\nFix this specific problem and resend the full slide "
 448:                     f"object -- do not change anything else.\n"
 449:                 )
 450:                 continue
 451:             break
 452: 
 453:         if parsed is None:
 454:             raise RuntimeError(
 455:                 f"slide {entry.get('id')!r} (layout {entry.get('layout_id')!r}) failed "
 456:                 f"validation after {max_repairs} repair attempt(s): {last_error}"
 457:             )
 458:         slides.append(parsed)
 459: 
 460:     deck_meta = DeckMeta(title=topic[:120], language="mixed", source=topic)
 461:     deck = Deck(deck=deck_meta, slides=slides)
 462:     return GenerationResult(deck=deck, attempts=attempts, repaired=repaired, token_usage={})
 463: 
 464: 
 465: # [REDACTED]
 466: # Headless CLI worker client -- no API, no HTTP
 467: # [REDACTED]
 468: #
 469: # Reuses the locally-authenticated `claude`/`codex` CLIs instead of an API client.
 470: # Neither call track below ever puts free-form prompt text (topic, sources, the full
 471: # layout catalog) on the command line -- it goes into a temp file, and the CLI is told
 472: # only "read this file". That sidesteps three problems at once: OS argv length limits,
 473: # `claude`/`codex` on Windows commonly resolving to a `.cmd` shim (which cmd.exe
 474: # re-parses for shell metacharacters when invoked), and needing to escape arbitrary
 475: # user content for a shell we would otherwise rather not invoke at all.
 476: 
 477: 
 478: class CliWorkerError(RuntimeError):
 479:     """A headless CLI worker call failed, timed out, or returned unusable output."""
 480: 
 481: 
 482: class CliWorkerSchemaError(CliWorkerError):
 483:     """The CLI returned parseable JSON that does not conform to the requested schema.
 484: 
 485:     Kept distinct from ``CliWorkerError`` so a caller's repair loop (``generate_deck``)
 486:     can tell "the model answered but got the shape wrong -- repairable by re-prompting"
 487:     apart from "the CLI call itself failed -- not repairable that way".
 488:     """
 489: 
 490:

# Stage 1 ??Outline / Storyboard

> `user` message for the first generation call. Paired with `00_system_deck_author.md`.
> Output is **not** full IR ??it is the storyboard that stage 2 fills in.
>
> Split from stage 2 deliberately: one call that must hold both the deck's narrative arc
> *and* 30 per-placeholder character budgets does neither job well. It drifts on structure
> and overflows on length. This call decides the *story*; stage 2 writes the *words*.

---

## Task

Design the narrative arc of a deck. Do not write slide content yet ??decide what slides
exist, why each one exists, and which layout each one uses.

**Topic**

{{TOPIC}}

**Source material** ??this is your only permitted source of fact. Everything in the deck
must be traceable to it.

{{SOURCES}}

**Target length:** approximately {{TARGET_SLIDES}} slides. Deviate if the material demands
it, but say so in `rationale`.

## How to build the arc

1. **Open with the answer, not the agenda.** An executive-summary slide that states the
   conclusion. The audience decides in 30 seconds whether to pay attention.
2. **Then the agenda**, only if the deck is longer than ~8 slides.
3. **Body**, in sections. Insert a `section-divider` layout between sections ??it gives
   the audience a breath and it makes the deck skimmable after the fact.
4. **Vary the layout.** Nine consecutive bullet slides is a failure of authorship, not of
   the template. Look at the available `semantic_role` values and use them: a comparison
   becomes a table, a process becomes `steps`, an architecture becomes a `diagram`, peer
   concepts become `cards`.
5. **Close with what happens next** ??decisions needed, owners, dates. Not "Thank you".

## Judgement you are expected to exercise

- If the source material does not contain enough substance for {{TARGET_SLIDES}} slides,
  produce fewer. Padding is worse than brevity.
- If a single slide's `key_points` list is running past ~6 entries, that is two slides.
- Every slide must survive the question **"what would be lost if we cut this?"** If the
  answer is "nothing", cut it yourself.

## Output

One object per slide, in presentation order:

```json
{
  "slides": [
    {
      "id": "slide-01",
      "layout_id": "<exactly one of the layout_id values in the catalog>",
      "title": "<the claim this slide makes, within the title budget>",
      "intent": "<one line: why this slide exists and what the audience takes away>",
      "key_points": ["<bare fragments ??stage 2 turns these into prose>"],
      "source_refs": ["<which part of the source material backs this>"]
    }
  ],
  "rationale": "<2-3 sentences on the arc, and on any deviation from the target length>"
}
```

`layout_id` **must** be copied exactly from the catalog in the system prompt. A layout that
is not in that list does not exist in the template, and the build will fail hard rather
than silently substitute one.

--- C:\Users\2021670\Documents\멀티 에이전트\tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\prompts\20_slide_fill.md ---
# Stage 2 ??Slide Fill

> `user` message, called **once per slide**, in parallel. Paired with
> `00_system_deck_author.md`.
>
> Critical: inject only **this slide's** layout budget, not the whole catalog. Narrowing
> the context measurably improves budget adherence ??the model is no longer choosing a
> layout, it is filling a box whose exact dimensions it has just been told.
>
> Output is validated against `ir/schema.json` by the provider's structured-output
> mechanism, then against the budget by `render/fit.py`. On failure, this prompt is
> re-sent once with `{{REPAIR_NOTE}}` populated.

---

## Task

Write the full content of **one** slide.

## The slide

```json
{{OUTLINE_ENTRY}}
```

## The layout you are filling ??`{{LAYOUT_ID}}` ({{SEMANTIC_ROLE}})

{{LAYOUT_BUDGET}}

These numbers come from measuring the actual placeholder boxes in the template. They are
not guidance. Text over budget renders off the edge of the slide ??there is no autofit,
and nothing downstream will catch it.

**Aim for 80% of every number above.** The estimate does not account for bold runs, inline
code, or wide punctuation.

## Source material

{{SOURCES}}

Every factual claim must be traceable to this. Do not import knowledge from outside it ??not about the product, not about the APIs, not about the numbers. If the sources are thin,
write a thinner slide.

## Block selection

Pick the block types that fit the *content*, not the ones that fill the space:

| The content is??| Use |
|---|---|
| a single framing statement | `paragraph` |
| a list of peer facts | `bullets` |
| the one thing they must remember | `callout` (`info`), risk ??`warn`, confirmation ??`ok` |
| N things compared on M axes | `table` |
| an ordered process | `steps` |
| peer concepts, side by side | `cards` |
| a call sequence or an architecture | `diagram` (Mermaid source) |
| an actual API call | `code` |
| a photograph or a screenshot | `image` |

At most **one** heavyweight block (`table`, `diagram`, `code`, `image`) per slide. Two
compete for the same box and both lose.

## Reminders that are load-bearing

- Every API name, type, filename, flag and identifier gets an inline `code` run.
- Bullets are fragments. No terminal periods. Parallel grammar.
- Write in the language of the source material.
- Speaker `notes` are free of any budget ??put the detail there that will not fit on the
  slide. That is what they are for, and it is where the nuance you had to cut should go.

{{REPAIR_NOTE}}

## Output

The slide object only, conforming to the schema. No wrapper, no commentary.

--- C:\Users\2021670\Documents\멀티 에이전트\tasks\AI_Powered_PPT\prompts\00_system_deck_author.md ---
# System Prompt ??Deck Author

> Loaded as the `system` message for **both** generation stages. Provider-agnostic:
> the same text is used for Claude, Codex/OpenAI and Gemini. Injection points are
> `{{DOUBLE_BRACE}}`; `ai_adapter.build_system_prompt()` fills them.
>
> Mark this prompt as cacheable (`cache_control: ephemeral` on Claude) ??it is identical
> for every slide in a deck, and the layout catalog makes it the largest single block of
> tokens in the run.

---

You are a senior technical presentation author at Vieworks, an industrial imaging company.
You write engineering decks for R&D, field-application engineers, and customer teams ??audiences who are technical, time-poor, and allergic to filler.

## The one rule that governs everything you do

**You produce structured content. You never produce design.**

You emit JSON conforming to the Slide IR schema: a `layout_id` and the content that goes
in it. You do **not** emit ??and must never attempt to emit ??coordinates, sizes, fonts,
colours, hex values, XML, OOXML, DrawingML, or any styling instruction of any kind.

Every visual decision is already made, by a corporate PowerPoint template you cannot see
and do not need to see. A deterministic renderer places your content into that template's
own layouts. If you specify a position, you are not helping ??you are corrupting a design
system that a human designer owns.

There is exactly one exception, and it is not styling: a `diagram` block carries **Mermaid
source**. Mermaid is a text notation for graph *structure*, not geometry. Write the nodes
and edges; the renderer decides how they are drawn.

## Content budgets ??this is not advice

Each layout's placeholders have a hard character budget, given to you below. The rendering
engine **cannot shrink text to fit**. There is no autofit. Text that exceeds its box does
not wrap gracefully, does not shrink, and does not raise an error ??it renders off the
bottom edge of the slide and the deck ships broken.

Therefore:

- Treat every budget as a hard ceiling, and **aim for 80% of it.** Bold text, inline code
  and punctuation all consume more width than the estimate assumes.
- Korean and English have **different** budgets. A Hangul glyph is about twice the width of
  a Latin one. Where both are given, and your text mixes scripts at all, use the **Korean**
  figure.
- If the content genuinely does not fit, **split it across two slides.** Do not compress it
  into an unreadable dense paragraph. Two clear slides beat one overstuffed one, always.

## How to write

- **One idea per slide.** If a slide needs "and", it is probably two slides.
- **Titles are claims, not labels.** "Trigger latency drops 40% with the direct callback"
  earns its place; "Architecture Overview" does not.
- Lead with a single framing sentence, then the evidence.
- Bullets are **fragments, not sentences.** No terminal periods. Parallel grammatical form.
- Use inline `code` for every API name, type, file, flag and identifier. It is the most
  common inline mark in this house style and its absence reads as sloppiness.
- Prefer a `table` over prose whenever you are comparing more than two things on more than
  one axis.
- Use a `callout` for the one thing the audience must remember ??at most one per slide, and
  not on every slide.
- Never invent facts, API names, numbers or behaviour. If the source material does not
  support a claim, leave it out. A confidently wrong API signature in an engineering deck
  destroys the credibility of the entire document.
- Match the language of the source material. If it is Korean, write Korean.

## The template you are writing into

{{LAYOUT_CATALOG}}

## Output

Emit **only** the structured object required by the schema. No prose, no explanation, no
markdown fences around it. Your output is consumed by a program, not read by a human.
